**Authorized Scope**: TASKS only. Produce this bounded execution list and its briefs for P07; every task below describes future authorized implementation or verification and starts unchecked.

Apply the [constitution](../../.specify/memory/constitution.md), [engineering index](../../docs/engineering/README.md), [phase delivery](../../docs/workflow/phase-delivery.md) and [implementation handoff](../../docs/workflow/implementation-handoff.md). These delivery groups belong to P07 and do not add roadmap phases. Generation does not approve requirements checklists, implement code, apply migrations, execute application tests, accept the backend/phase or authorize a release. The existing roadmap, accepted product/design documents, both checklists and active feature selection are preserved. All new application/schema/test owners below are **proposed outputs of the named tasks**, not files claimed present at generation. Existing files are explicitly identified in the briefs; reopen them before execution and account for completed prerequisites.

## Task Format and Execution Rules

Checklist lines use `- [ ] T### [P?] [US#?] Description with exact path`. IDs are unique and stable. Story labels identify their primary outcome; the coverage table identifies secondary outcomes and shared foundations. `[P]` applies only to the explicit dependency window in the safe-execution section. All other shared-file work is sequential.

Each ID maps to a filled brief under **Task Briefs**. The brief's allowlist, prerequisites, read bundle, algorithm and checks constrain execution. Paths are repository-relative; package-relative test arguments are intentional. Read source and manifests again when resuming. Existing auth, transport, validation, database, QueryClient, forms, test isolation and Arabic/RTL/Cairo presentation are reused. No new dependency, courier service, inventory platform, partial delivery, finance policy or P08 surface is included.

The confirmed decisions are fixed: selecting piece rows expands to reviewed whole active orders before delivery actions; any invalid batch member or required-save failure rejects the entire batch; warehouse delivery contacts are submitted name/phone/address only. Receipt counts initial physical arrival once. Returns/retries do not recount it. Current 199/200 is 99%, exact positive equality is 100%, and zero active pieces are non-deliverable. Retained historical count facts remain unchanged. References below to rejecting stale state or old attempts concern new intents. An originating actor replaying the exact accepted command key/body receives its immutable original result after current authority is rechecked, even when the live order has since advanced; that historical result never replaces current detail.

### Common evidence and write boundaries

During later IMPLEMENT, each task records actual changed paths, command/results, fresh/cached/reused evidence, cleanup and separate REQUIREMENTS / ENGINEERING STANDARDS verdicts in this file's execution-evidence section. Mark only verified tasks complete. Documentation/check-only tasks modify their own marker/evidence; application corrections return to their owning in-scope implementation task. Preserve previous evidence and completed IDs. Material behavior/interface gaps return to the owning authorized planning step; routine syntax within a fixed design needs no extra approval.

All database claims use owned migrated PostgreSQL through the established integration harness. All HTTP claims use actual `createApp` and protected middleware. Hook/form claims use real QueryClient/RHF with transport doubles outside the claimed boundary. Browser claims use the owned real-stack Playwright harness. Missing infrastructure leaves the relevant gate open. Test setup must never fall back to developer/production data, broaden cleanup with CASCADE, stop unowned processes/containers, weaken assertions or add arbitrary sleeps/retries. No application command in a brief was executed by TASKS.

### Story completion and independent acceptance

All stories are P1. Their independent criteria mean a specific observable assertion with prepared authoritative prerequisites; they do not waive backend/frontend gates or imply separately releasable phases.

| Story                                | Dependency and independently observable acceptance                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Primary tasks                             |
| ------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| US1 — exact physical arrival         | P06 accepts P1/P2 with P3 upstream. Receive P1 using carton `2`, location `Shelf B-12` and note `Protect finished surfaces`; another actor reloads one receipt/group and received1/active3, with P2 incoming/P3 upstream, packing/paid unchanged. Lost-reply replay creates no second receipt.                          | T013, T035, T047                          |
| US2 — reconcile incomplete/ready     | Depends on initial receipt facts. Original4/cancelled1/active3/received2 is incomplete; the last receipt makes READY_FOR_DELIVERY. 199/200 displays99 and cannot dispatch; all-cancelled never dispatches. List/detail/dashboard/shared tracking agree and dispatched/failed/delivered leave incomplete.                | T014, T015, T036, T037, T038              |
| US3 — whole-order dispatch           | Depends on positive exact readiness. Selecting P1 of ready O1/P1/P2 reviews both; unsent cancel writes nothing. Confirm creates one full attempt. A stale second order rejects both orders entirely, including notes/events.                                                                                            | T016, T039, T048                          |
| US4 — complete customer receipt      | Depends on a current dispatched attempt (US3). Confirm all pieces using recipient `Test Recipient`, actual time `2026-11-13 14:30 +02:00` and note `Handed to recipient`; another actor reloads the same outcome. Optional blanks remain absent; subsets, old attempts and competing failure conflict unchanged.        | T017, T040; T048 also verifies this story |
| US5 — physical return and retry      | Depends on a current dispatched attempt (US3). Missing return acknowledgement/blank reason saves nothing. Full return with reason `Customer unavailable` records failed custody without recounting receipt. Retry with/without date restores READY_FOR_DELIVERY only; ordinary redispatch/success retains two attempts. | T018, T019, T041, T049                    |
| US6 — access, atomicity and recovery | Cross-cutting across all stories. Protected direct requests enforce current authority and minimal contacts; races/induced last-save failure preserve atomicity; dirty stale review, unknown/closed recovery, authority/late-response fencing and Arabic actionable failures remain truthful.                            | T020–T025, T042–T045, T050                |

### Implementation strategy and MVP

Use dependency-safe bounded batches, verifying and reviewing each responsibility before its consumers. First complete T001–T026, including real constraints, migrations, all five command operations, protected reads/HTTP and full backend acceptance. Then build frontend foundations and receipt/reconciliation surfaces in order. The first useful observable MVP slice is **US1 + US2: separate physical arrivals with truthful incomplete/ready reconciliation**, after the full backend gate; this is an internal acceptance checkpoint and does not waive the remaining P07 stories. Dispatch, receipt outcomes and failure/retry follow through existing surfaces, then cross-cutting recovery/history and real browser/exit evidence. No code batch, green focused suite or MVP checkpoint claims whole-phase or release acceptance.

### T005 — Four core and receipt models

**Dependencies / outcome**: T003, T004. FR-005–008/020–022; VR-002/006–007. Add the exact core receipt ledger and begin the single unapplied forward migration.

**Allowed file actions / symbols**: MODIFY `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` for WarehouseCommand, WarehouseCommandOrder, WarehouseReceiptGroup, WarehouseReceiptPiece, WarehouseOperation, WarehouseReceiptOrigin and necessary User/SalesOrder/SalesOrderPiece/QualityPieceEvent inverse relations; CREATE `packages/database/prisma/migrations/20261113000100_p07_warehouse_receipt_delivery/migration.sql` core enum/table/key/index contribution only.

**Read before edit**: BC0 and G-D; Data D01–D04; Contract C03–C04/C06. Inspect actual root/version/receivedAt and P06 composite acceptance event keys. The following D02 quote binds types, fields, nullability, identities and keys; do not choose alternatives.

**Binding Data D02 field contract (verbatim):**

```markdown
All names and mapped tables below are proposed additions to `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma`. UUIDs are PostgreSQL UUID, instants Timestamptz(6), date-only `Date`, finite strings VarChar as specified; all FKs use RESTRICT/NO ACTION and retain records. `ActorStamp` below expands to actorId FK User, actorFullName VarChar150, actorRole UserRole and nullable actorDepartment Department. Real command actors must be ADMIN or WAREHOUSE in SQL checks. It is a field shorthand in this document, **not** a new Prisma embedded type/table.

### WarehouseCommand → `warehouse_commands`

- commandId UUID primary key; operation new `WarehouseOperation` enum with C03's five values.
- ActorStamp, payloadHash Char64 lowercase SHA-256, occurredAt nonnull, outcome JsonB.
- `@@index([actorId,occurredAt(sort:Desc),commandId(sort:Desc)], map:"warehouse_commands_actor_time_idx")`.
- Immutable INSERT-only root, no update/delete/expiry/cleanup policy. Outcome is a safe immutable command projection validated against relational members, never the independent lifecycle ledger.

### WarehouseCommandOrder → `warehouse_command_orders`

- commandId, orderId UUID; composite PK `(commandId,orderId)`, FK command + SalesOrder.
- eventId UUID unique, beforeVersion Int, afterVersion Int, beforeStatus/afterStatus existing SalesOrderStatus, pieceIds UUID[] containing exact sorted distinct nonempty scope.
- unique `(orderId,afterVersion)`; index `(orderId,eventId)`.
- `afterVersion=beforeVersion+1` in BIGINT arithmetic, both 1–2147483647; max200 distinct IDs; event UUID stable. One row per affected order, one event even for200 receipt pieces. Root command across all groups totals1–200 pieces and1–200 orders.
- Immutable per-order version/event witness. FK `(commandId,orderId)` is the shared child key below; add corresponding composite unique keys where a child relation needs an ID+order reference.

### WarehouseReceiptGroup → `warehouse_receipt_groups`

- id UUID PK, commandId/orderId UUID unique pair and FK CommandOrder; unique `(id,orderId)`.
- cartonCount, storageLocation, notes nullable VarChar4000; blank forbidden after normalization, original significant text retained.
- No own fake worker/time: receipt actor/time is the parent command. One group per order in RECEIVE even when all optional fields absent. Distinct arrivals create distinct groups; group metadata is not copied per piece.
- A group belongs only to operation RECEIVE; each group must contain at least one matching COMMAND receipt member.

### WarehouseReceiptPiece → `warehouse_receipt_pieces`

- pieceId UUID PK: **one initial receipt witness per physical piece for its entire lifetime**.
- orderId UUID; FK `(pieceId,orderId)` to SalesOrderPiece `(id,orderId)`.
- origin new `WarehouseReceiptOrigin` enum COMMAND or LEGACY_BASELINE.
- groupId UUID nullable, FK `(groupId,orderId)` to ReceiptGroup `(id,orderId)`; qualityEventId UUID nullable, FK `(qualityEventId,orderId,pieceId)` to QualityPieceEvent `(id,orderId,pieceId)`.
- receivedAt nonnull; index `(orderId,groupId,pieceId)`; unique `(pieceId,orderId)` for attempt-member relations.
- COMMAND requires groupId and qualityEventId, exact source event is ACCEPT/QUALITY→WAREHOUSE and source parent INSPECT, source time≤receivedAt. Parent receipt command time equals receivedAt; command scope contains exactly these members. LEGACY_BASELINE requires groupId/qualityEventId null; it carries the pre-existing known timestamp only. It exposes no fabricated actor/packing/acceptance witness.
- Install runtime INSERT guard after migration baseline insertion to reject every new LEGACY_BASELINE member. All receipt members immutable. Presence of a group ID cannot bypass physical transition predicates.
```

**Ordered steps**: (1) Add exact models/enums/inverse relations. (2) Add matching mapped DDL, CHECK/UNIQUE/FKs/indexes in the new migration under assembly. (3) Retain group linkage and parent actor/time ownership; add no duplicate snapshot/root. (4) Compare Prisma/SQL with every quoted field and C04 result identity. Leave baseline/guards/final COMMIT to T007–T009.

**Preserve / failures unchanged**: BC0 unapplied-migration boundary. Existing hashes, receivedAt physical meaning, IDs and money remain. Receipt uniqueness is lifelong, not per attempt. No fake legacy actor/group/source or client-data action.

**Verification / done**: Use scoped `git diff --check -- packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma packages/database/prisma/migrations/20261113000100_p07_warehouse_receipt_delivery/migration.sql` and exact Prisma/SQL correspondence review. Schema-only `pnpm --filter @template/database db:validate` may run only when the assembled Prisma model is internally complete; it applies no SQL. No integration/global setup/deploy until T009. T010 owns real DB results; record this as a reviewed bounded assembly contribution. BC0 supplies infrastructure/cleanup and separate requirements/engineering review obligations. Record only the boundary actually checked; no whole-backend, frontend or release acceptance is implied.

### T006 — Four immutable delivery models

**Dependencies / outcome**: T005. FR-011–020/023; VR-004–007. Add exact complete-order attempt/member/outcome/retry graph without mutable latest-only evidence.

**Allowed file actions / symbols**: MODIFY only `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma` and `packages/database/prisma/migrations/20261113000100_p07_warehouse_receipt_delivery/migration.sql` for WarehouseDeliveryAttempt, WarehouseDeliveryAttemptMember, WarehouseDeliveryOutcome, WarehouseDeliveryRetry, WarehouseDeliveryOutcomeKind and required inverse relations.

**Read before edit**: BC0 and G-D; Data D01–D03 and quoted D02 delivery contract; Contract C03/C06–C07. Inspect actual SalesOrderStatus/SalesPieceStage and completed T005 core relations. Initial receipt is not repeated on later delivery attempts.

**Binding Data D02 field contract (verbatim):**

```markdown
All names and mapped tables below are proposed additions to `packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma`. UUIDs are PostgreSQL UUID, instants Timestamptz(6), date-only `Date`, finite strings VarChar as specified; all FKs use RESTRICT/NO ACTION and retain records. `ActorStamp` below expands to actorId FK User, actorFullName VarChar150, actorRole UserRole and nullable actorDepartment Department. Real command actors must be ADMIN or WAREHOUSE in SQL checks. It is a field shorthand in this document, **not** a new Prisma embedded type/table.

### WarehouseDeliveryAttempt → `warehouse_delivery_attempts`

- id UUID PK, orderId UUID, number Int positive; unique `(orderId,number)` and `(id,orderId)`.
- commandId UUID; unique `(commandId,orderId)`, FK CommandOrder. deliveryNote nullable VarChar4000.
- predecessorRetryId UUID nullable unique, FK Retry `(id,orderId)`; first number1 has null predecessor; each later number binds the immediately preceding attempt's failed outcome + retry.
- Index `(orderId,number(sort:Desc))` supports latest attempt without loading all history.
- Actor/time comes from dispatch parent command. Attempt is immutable original dispatch, **no mutable status column**. DTO status is derived from unique outcome. Later outcome/retry does not update dispatch record or its event projection.

### WarehouseDeliveryAttemptMember → `warehouse_delivery_attempt_members`

- attemptId/orderId/pieceId UUID; PK `(attemptId,pieceId)`.
- FKs `(attemptId,orderId)` to Attempt and `(pieceId,orderId)` to ReceiptPiece; index `(orderId,pieceId,attemptId)`.
- Exact original full active membership, max200. Different attempts may refer to the same received piece; one attempt may not repeat it. Insert-only.

### WarehouseDeliveryOutcome → `warehouse_delivery_outcomes`

- id UUID PK, attemptId/orderId UUID; attemptId unique, FK `(attemptId,orderId)`; unique `(id,orderId)`.
- commandId UUID, unique `(commandId,orderId)`, FK CommandOrder.
- kind new `WarehouseDeliveryOutcomeKind` enum DELIVERED or FAILED.
- recipient nullable VarChar4000, deliveredAt nullable Timestamptz(6), reason nullable VarChar4000, notes nullable VarChar4000, returnedToWarehouse Boolean.
- DELIVERED requires returnedToWarehouse false, reason null; optional recipient/actual deliveredAt retained. FAILED requires returnedToWarehouse true, nonblank reason, recipient/deliveredAt null. Actor/recording time comes from parent command and is separate from actual deliveredAt.
- A unique attemptId makes success-versus-failure mutually exclusive even across different command keys. One immutable outcome; no edits or replacement by a later response.

### WarehouseDeliveryRetry → `warehouse_delivery_retries`

- id UUID PK, attemptId/orderId UUID, outcomeId UUID; attemptId unique; FK `(attemptId,orderId)` to Attempt and `(outcomeId,orderId)` to Outcome; unique `(id,orderId)`.
- commandId UUID; unique `(commandId,orderId)`, FK CommandOrder; retryDate nullable PostgreSQL Date and notes nullable VarChar4000.
- Outcome must belong to that same attempt and be FAILED. Actor/time comes from retry command, at or after failed recording time. One immutable retry per failed attempt; ordinary next dispatch references it exactly once. No automatic dispatch or overwrite of submitted promised date.
```

**Ordered steps**: (1) Add four exact models/FKs/uniques/indexes and parent stamp relations. (2) Keep attempts immutable with status derived from unique outcome; each later attempt references the preceding failed attempt retry exactly once. (3) Add row-local operation/nullability DDL; T007 supplies full graph validators. (4) Use deferrable complete transaction relationships, never intermediate commits or fabricated parent rows.

**Preserve / failures unchanged**: No new status/count ledger, piece delivery stage or fake courier. Optional recipient/actual time remains absent. Unique attempt outcome forbids simultaneous terminal results; one retry has at most one successor. BC0 migration remains unapplied.

**Verification / done**: Run scoped `git diff --check -- packages/database/prisma/schema.prisma packages/database/prisma/migrations/20261113000100_p07_warehouse_receipt_delivery/migration.sql`; compare every quoted Prisma/SQL field/relationship. Schema-only `pnpm --filter @template/database db:validate` is permitted for a complete Prisma schema, not migration application. T010/T022 later prove actual FK/sequence/rollback behavior. BC0 supplies infrastructure/cleanup and separate requirements/engineering review obligations. Record only the boundary actually checked; no whole-backend, frontend or release acceptance is implied.

### T049 — Full-return failure, optional-date retry and redispatch history

**Outcome and traceability.** Cover US5; FR-016–024/026–027, VR-005–009, SC-005–008. Prove delivery failure explicitly means all dispatched goods physically returned, retry restores ready without dispatch, and a subsequent ordinary dispatch/new outcome retains every earlier attempt.

**Allowed file actions.** MODIFY `apps/web/e2e/warehouse-receipt-delivery.spec.ts` for T049 scenarios/local helpers and T049 evidence/verified marker in `specs/007-warehouse-receipt-delivery/tasks.md`. **Read-before-edit context.** `spec.md` US5, FR-016–019, failure table and D-005; `backend-design.md` W03/W05/W06/W07 and B02 cases B06–B09; `contracts/warehouse.md` C02–C04/C06–C08; `data-model.md` unique attempt outcome/retry/successor and unchanged initial receipt; `frontend-design.md` whole-order review, return acknowledgement, retry field mapping and state recovery. Apply testing and test-guard requirements for meaningful valid/invalid case assertions and exact owned teardown.

**Ordered steps.**

1. Start from a real ready order, dispatch through the UI, and read its current attempt plus initial physical receipt count/groups. Open failure and assert the action explains that all goods are back in warehouse. Without the explicit acknowledgement, or with blank/whitespace reason, assert field/action-associated Arabic guidance, retained entered notes and zero saved failure outcome. A quick-fill is valid only after explicit selection; no generic reason may be fabricated.
2. Acknowledge full physical return and enter reason `Customer unavailable` plus optional notes. Submit the whole current attempt scope. Parse the real body/result, then reload and assert DELIVERY_FAILED, exactly one FAILED outcome, returned custody and the original initial received count/groups/manufacturing/price/paid evidence unchanged.
3. From failed view and shared detail assert direct dispatch is unavailable; exercise a real invalid direct failed-to-dispatch request with unchanged state. Open retry, leave date blank and enter notes. Confirm and assert READY_FOR_DELIVERY with a retained retry record but no new attempt/dispatch and no extra physical receipt. The existing promised delivery date is not cleared or rewritten.
4. Dispatch ordinarily again; assert the next attempt number and exact predecessor retry, then complete another returned failure. Retry this time for `2026-11-15` with distinct notes, confirming date-only preservation. Ordinary next dispatch followed by customer receipt succeeds. This repeated cycle exercises retry both with and without a date without inventing date restrictions or timezone inference.
5. Read all attempt/history pages, not just the current attempt. Assert original dispatch events remain dispatched action-time snapshots, failed reasons/notes/actors/times and each retry stay attached to the correct attempt, and final delivered status does not overwrite earlier outcome evidence. Validate a new intent targeting an earlier attempt is denied and does not alter final state; replay of an already accepted original intent remains its immutable historical result.

**Invariants and failures.** The acknowledged return is employee testimony, not sensor/courier proof; the test cannot pretend goods returned from an unrelated network failure. No partial return/delivery platform, automatic dispatch, second initial receipt, backdated server actor or overwritten captured field is introduced. Empty retry date is valid; malformed supplied calendar date must retain draft and leave failed state unchanged. Use actual persisted reads after the real UI actions; never fulfil the happy path with synthetic JSON. Close all owned contexts and settle/remove task-owned gates/routes/listeners in `finally`.

**Verification and done.** Use stable `P07 US5` names. Run both harness typechecks, then `pnpm --filter @template/web test:e2e warehouse-receipt-delivery.spec.ts --grep 'P07 US5'` on both configured projects with normal no-retry settings. Record discovered cases, captured real command/outcome/attempt identities, unchanged initial receipt and prior evidence, warnings and cleanup. Done requires invalid acknowledgement/reason handling, explicit retry sequencing, both date variants, repeated retained attempts and final success; T052 owns the unfiltered complete browser regression.

Read actual new warehouse schema, migration/head/transition guards, command/read/wire/UI owners and immediate P03/P04/P05/P06 callers, safe projection/command recovery and tests. Consult installed Next docs for affected framework boundaries. Apply matching clean-code-guard, test-guard, docs-guard and React guidance to their actual scopes; do not claim a skill review merely by naming it.

**Ordered review.**

1. Reconcile current Git status/branch/revision and preserved/unowned edits against intake; name what was included/excluded. Verify root roadmap, earlier accepted decisions/IDs, original migration history and P07 spec/custom/built-in checklist markers were preserved according to their owners.
2. Trace each W01–W08 operation and failure branch through input/validation/current authority/lock/read-or-transaction/explicit projection/HTTP/schema/adapter/query or form/reached UI. Review exact receipt once, positive all-active readiness, full reviewed scope, all-or-nothing outcomes, current attempt/full-return/retry sequence, capture/actor/history and immutable original intent. Compare actual source with fixed plan decisions rather than assuming neighboring legacy shortcuts comply.
3. Review shared integration separately: capped current99 versus retained100 without old JSON rewrites; current-cache refetch/version floors; forward P04/P05/P06 witnesses and untouched prior migrations; last-missing cancellation readiness and paid/snapshot retention; correct current custody and warehouse history discriminator; inverse and forward authorized invalidation; factory privacy/official print boundaries. Check body bounds/413, sparse multi-root work and both maximum command cases against their actual earlier evidence.
4. Review test meaning/discovery/boundaries/cleanup and all required negative/race/rollback/unknown cases. A mocked transaction, hidden field, screenshot, runner start, cached count or sequential replay alone proves no wider claim. Check no skipped assertions, retry/sleep masks, arbitrary type suppression, weakened guards or credentials in artifacts. Confirm technical documentation/OpenAPI/example/command claims match current producers and consumers.
5. Record each finding with path/symbol or line, owning FR/guide, consequence, smallest in-scope correction and verification. Resolve required findings through owning tasks with source-matching focused checks; then record separate REQUIREMENTS and ENGINEERING STANDARDS PASS/BLOCKED verdicts. Preserve unsuccessful evidence and unperformed boundaries. Do not infer production client/OPS-01 activation, full phase acceptance or deployed release.

**Verification and done.** No ceremonial application rerun is required solely for reading a diff. Reuse matching T002–T050 evidence; new findings/fixes require the relevant focused checks at their actual boundary. Documentation evidence uses scoped format/link/reference checks, not `pnpm verify`. T051 is done only when all in-scope responsibilities/callers have concrete coverage, all required findings are resolved, and both verdicts with evidence limits are recorded in `tasks.md`. Checklists remain read-only during code/exit review. Full whole-phase execution and cleanup remain T052.

### T052 — Full local P07 exit checks, owned cleanup and evidence

**Outcome and traceability.** Shared local phase exit for FR-001–027, VR-001–010, SC-001–008, with actual source-matching checks and no unresolved required finding. Distinguish backend acceptance, frontend/browser acceptance, local P07 completion and production/release status. No previous task count or partial suite closes this gate.

**Dependencies.** T051 and every preceding required task/gate. Verify the current selected feature/task scope and latest source before starting commands. The full SQL migration was completed before its earlier application/integration gate; this task must not deploy an unfinished migration or use an unrelated developer/production target.

**Allowed file actions.** MODIFY `specs/007-warehouse-receipt-delivery/tasks.md` for exact command/results/cleanup/review/remaining gates and the verified T052 marker. RUN only the accepted existing full verification/harness commands below during later authorized implementation. These commands create their normal build/generated/test outputs and owned disposable resources; preserve existing unowned edits/resources. No source/spec/design/checklist rewrite, manual business action, catalog import, credential request, production migration, commit/push or deployment. A failed required check returns to its owning task and keeps this exit open.

**Read-before-execution context.** `plan.md` “Verification and Review Plan”, “Maximum and sparse command cases”, “Infrastructure, fault injection and cleanup”, “Planned phase-exit checks and reviews”, “Design Readiness and Exit”; `backend-design.md` B02/B03; `data-model.md` D04/D06; `frontend-design.md` section9; `docs/engineering/testing.md` full execution/cleanup/evidence rules; actual root/package manifests, Turbo configuration, integration global setups, Playwright config/runtime/global setup and current build-output checker. Read T026/T046 and T047–T051 evidence; ensure no product/interface decision is missing. Source/API/database migration claims require their real boundaries, not this document's planned commands.

**Ordered checks and completion.**

1. Record current branch/revision/Git state and confirm meaningful earlier focused/shared suites, fresh/populated disposable migration, negative SQL, cross-module root guards, current/retained compatibility, controlled races/rollback, one-order200 and sparse200-root through-COMMIT cases have passing source-matching evidence. Missing required local Docker/Chromium or owned test-data identity blocks its corresponding local check; never use developer/production data as fallback. An unaudited live target's provenance or unknown pre-existing delivery states remain explicit target/release gates and do not invent a live-data audit prerequisite for local P07 acceptance.
2. Execute the existing accepted resource-bounded full sequence separately, inspect actual final output/discovery and record fresh versus cached results and warnings:

   ```sh
   pnpm format:check
   pnpm lint
   pnpm check-types
   pnpm test --concurrency=1 -- --maxWorkers=2
   pnpm test:integration --concurrency=1
   pnpm build
   pnpm verify:build-output
   pnpm --filter @template/web check:e2e-types
   pnpm --filter @template/api check:e2e-types
   pnpm --filter @template/web test:e2e
   git diff --check
   ```

   The final browser command is unfiltered and must include every discovered existing and new P07 journey on both configured projects. Do not substitute only a grep/name-filtered run, one viewport, a screenshot, harness typecheck or prior P06 pass. Root `pnpm verify` is not required as a second ritual; its schema formatting/generation side effects do not add evidence once the actual applicable sequence is complete.

3. After successful browser teardown restore normal configured production build output with `pnpm build`, run `pnpm verify:build-output` again and finish `git diff --check`. Label matching cache restoration accurately; do not claim a fresh rebuild where Turbo restored cache. Re-run a check only for a relevant fix/failure or source-state change, not to create repeated ceremonial passes.
4. Verify exact owned cleanup: integration clients/containers, race/fault resources and all browser contexts/children/private runtime artifacts must be settled/removed by their owning harness/finally paths. Remove only a successful run's exact generated artifacts where its ownership manifest and safe cleanup policy permit it. Preserve pre-existing developer processes/containers, unowned temporary files and required retained evidence; no wildcards or broad CASCADE/reset cleanup. Do not expose credentials or enable trace/video/screenshot captures.
5. Reconcile task markers only after outcomes are verified. Append concise actual commands, discovered counts/scenarios/roles, persisted outcomes, warnings, failed and superseding attempts, matching reused/cached evidence, cleanup, scoped final source state and distinct verdicts: REQUIREMENTS, ENGINEERING STANDARDS, BACKEND, FRONTEND, BROWSER and LOCAL P07 EXIT. Required failures remain visible and block their owning acceptance. Keep production activation/client-input/OPS-01/data-audit/release boundaries explicit; local completion does not establish deployment, live-mail, backup/restore or release approval.

**Invariants and done.** Do not weaken timeout/assertion/discovery/authorization/constraint/binder policies, remove tests, hide warnings, retry away failures or mark unperformed acceptance complete. No remaining required task, material design gap or in-scope finding may be waived because the run is expensive. T052 is done only after the full applicable source-matching sequence, required real boundary proofs, exact cleanup and all local verdicts pass with evidence recorded. Any unavailable infrastructure or unresolved required evidence leaves the specific gate and T052 incomplete. Commit/push/deployment and P08 remain separately authorized work.

## Phase 1: Convergence

**Assessment: 2026-11-15.** This is the first convergence section within existing P07, not a new roadmap phase or permission for P08. Preserve every earlier task, marker, brief and evidence entry. Convergence inspected current implementation and recorded evidence; no tests, builds or formatters ran. All1178 final-checkpoint paths matched the assessed source. Earlier passing suites remain evidence for the scenarios actually exercised, not proof of the interaction below.

**F1 — CRITICAL / contradicts.** Constitution VIII mandates preservation of existing interaction patterns; FR-001/027, VR-008/009 and SC-008 require working search and keyboard/focus behavior. `WarehouseQueue` keys the entire `WarehouseQueueContent` by view/search/type/mode/page (`warehouse-queue.tsx`, current lines65–70). Its subtree contains `WarehouseTable` and the actual search/type/mode/pagination controls. `OrientalFilterBar` immediately forwards every character. Each change therefore replaces the focused input node, interrupting ordinary multi-character typing; other context controls have the same remount cause. There is no autofocus/restoration mechanism. This finding is source-derived, not a newly executed browser failure or a claim of data corruption. Existing component searches make a single complete-value change; browser searches use `.fill(fullString)`, so they do not establish sequential-typing focus preservation.

Inventory assessed:27 FR,29 acceptance scenarios across6 stories,10 VR,8 SC,52 complete task briefs,7 plan decisions,8 operation blueprints,4 frontend algorithms and11 constitution principles. No other actionable backend, wire, persistence, recovery or evidence gap was identified. The new task below records remaining work without changing earlier completion claims.

**F2 — HIGH / partial.** T053 fixed steps5–6 and its absent/expired-metadata invariant are incomplete. In `apps/web/src/features/warehouse/components/warehouse-queue.tsx`, `previous` is derived by an imperative cache read (current lines122–131/241) and `pageChange` uses that render's captured bounds (136–145). After successful page1 supplies three-page metadata and page2 becomes pending or failed, removing/garbage-collecting the inactive page1 query does not notify Queue: its hooks observe page2, and Queue has no subscription to the remembered entry. The old pager can remain enabled and its existing handler can admit page3 after its metadata entry disappears. The current removal regression in `warehouse-workspace.test.tsx` explicitly forces `view.rerender` (line544), masking the absent production notification. Installed QueryClient documentation and QueryCache removal/observer sources confirm this event path. This is source-derived, not a newly executed failing test. Impact is stale navigation metadata/controls and an unsupported page GET; no old-row publication, authorization bypass, write permission or incorrect persisted outcome was established.

### T054 — Reactive paging-cache lifetime and event-time navigation fence

**Outcome / dependencies.** Depends on completed T053 and its owner-approved test-only HTTP isolation correction. Resolve F2: while the requested page is pending/failed, actual removal/expiry or invalid replacement of the remembered QueryClient entry withdraws its pager and last-confirmed label without an unrelated render. A page callback captured before batched notification must synchronously refuse navigation once neither current nor remembered canonical metadata is valid. Current valid page metadata retains precedence. This is a read-only frontend cache-observation/admission correction, with no product, HTTP, wire, persistent-state or command/recovery policy change. Apply the handoff/current-source gate before code writes; return genuinely material new gaps to their owning authorized step.

**Verified interface ledger.** React19.2.8/types19.2.18 provides `useSyncExternalStore<Snapshot>(subscribe: (onStoreChange: () => void) => () => void, getSnapshot: () => Snapshot, getServerSnapshot?: () => Snapshot): Snapshot`. Installed TanStack Query5.101.4 publicly reexports QueryCore through `@tanstack/react-query`: QueryCache `subscribe(listener: (event: QueryCacheNotifyEvent) => void): () => void`; each event supplies `event.type` and `event.query.queryKey`; `hashKey(QueryKey): string`; `notifyManager.batchCalls(callback)`; and existing QueryClient `getQueryData/getQueryCache`. Existing `readNavigation(client, scope, key, {ordersQuery,piecesQuery})` returns validated canonical pagination or null. These are reused interfaces, not new transport/public feature contracts. For deliberate cached-corruption regression setup only, actual Query objects expose `setState(state: Partial<QueryState<TData,TError>>): void` and can be obtained with `client.getQueryCache().find({queryKey, exact:true})`. Verify current installed signatures on resume; no guessed key-array positions or casts.

**Fixed implementation algorithm.**

1. Keep only the current `{kind,key} | null` remembered state. Retain actual list hooks/current body, controls and complete keyed transient subtree exactly as T053 requires. Never copy rows, full responses or pagination objects into React state/refs.
2. Compute the remembered key's `hashKey` string or null. Add a read-only component-local `subscribe` stabilized with `useCallback` and dependencies QueryClient plus that hash. With no remembered key, return a no-op unsubscribe. Otherwise create one `notifyManager.batchCalls(changed)` callback and subscribe to QueryCache. Invoke it only for `added`, `removed` or `updated` events whose `hashKey(event.query.queryKey)` matches the remembered hash. Return the actual unsubscribe. Do not mount another QueryObserver, fetch the old page, extend its gcTime or change global notification scheduling.
3. Give `useSyncExternalStore` a stable **string** snapshot derived on demand through existing `readNavigation` for the remembered key/current scope/canonical queries. Return one fixed `"unavailable"` sentinel when it returns null. For valid metadata, return `hashKey([remembered.kind, remembered.key, navigation])`. Use the unavailable sentinel for the server snapshot. Equal valid navigation must produce equal strings; removal, invalid/foreign replacement or changed valid pagination must change the token as applicable. This opaque derived token is only a notification/version signal: never decode it, retain separate pagination state or treat it as data authority. A valid replacement with identical navigation requires no display change.
4. Consume the snapshot in rendering: unavailable means no remembered fallback; otherwise read and validate that fallback again through QueryClient. Preserve current valid page metadata first and matching remembered fallback second. Keep current loading/error/retained-data semantics, requested page, Arabic last-confirmed label and fresh-success-only page clamping. Do not newly reject otherwise valid last-confirmed metadata merely because its entry is invalidated or stale; this correction concerns its actual validated cache lifetime.
5. Revalidate inside `pageChange` immediately before admission. Reread current-key metadata through `readNavigation` using the handler's canonical queries/scope; only if absent reread the remembered key. Do not trust the render's captured `previous` or an old success boolean. If neither is valid, clear the remembered key and return without changing controls or issuing a request. Validate integer page and bounds against the newly read metadata. Remember the current key only if its event-time result was valid, then use existing `change({page})`. The existing scope/key/schema checks remain authoritative; this synchronous gate closes the removal-to-render window left by batched notifications.
6. Preserve stable surviving control nodes, grouped limit10/flat25 and complete25-row rendering, search normalization/validation, selection/expansion/unsent-review resets, cap200, late-preparation fences, before-UUID synchronous review checks and original pending/unknown/confirmed intent recovery. The observer must never subscribe to or modify command-slot state. Actual removal of a pager/control is allowed; do not add autofocus/caret restoration or an IME workaround.

**Invariants / failure behavior.** QueryClient remains the server-data owner. Only validated same-context last-confirmed metadata can support fallback navigation. Absent/expired, malformed, foreign or removed metadata cannot continue admitting page requests while the next query is pending/failed. Observation must not keep inactive data alive or silently refresh it. A valid current result wins over an old entry even if the old entry later changes or disappears. No previous-page rows/count readiness/selection/actions are supplied as current results; a failed read never becomes empty success. Account/path/filter/mode changes and submitted-command recovery keep their accepted independent lifetimes. Unsubscription and every owned gate/context must settle in cleanup/finally on both success and failure.
